Enable Cluster Autoscaler on the node groups, or install Karpenter ([Cluster Autoscaler guide](/next/guides/cluster-autoscaler/)). 2. **What’s keeping the nodes?** BLOCKED BY in `kubectl napwake nodes`, with how to clear each below its table: | BLOCKED BY | Cause | | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `not autoscaled` | No autoscaler found scales it | | `NodePool never consolidates`, `NodePool consolidates only empty nodes` | Karpenter `consolidateAfter: Never`, or `consolidationPolicy: WhenEmpty` on a node with more than DaemonSets | | `scale-down disabled`, `do-not-disrupt on the node` | Node annotated `cluster-autoscaler.kubernetes.io/scale-down-disabled: "true"` or `karpenter.sh/do-not-disrupt: "true"` | | `PDB allows no disruption` | A pod whose PodDisruptionBudget allows none | | `safe-to-evict=false`, `do-not-disrupt` | Pod annotated `cluster-autoscaler.kubernetes.io/safe-to-evict: "false"` or `karpenter.sh/do-not-disrupt: "true"` | | `local storage` | A pod with `emptyDir` or `hostPath`, kept while Cluster Autoscaler’s `--skip-nodes-with-local-storage` is on | | `bare pod` | A pod no controller would recreate | | `kube-system pod without PDB` | Kept while `--skip-nodes-with-system-pods` is on | 3. **Busy neighbours?** A node goes only once everything on it can, so a cluster of slightly busy nodes keeps them all. Check UTILISATION in `kubectl napwake nodes`. Low, with nothing in BLOCKED BY, means the node awaits the autoscaler’s threshold or delay. Middling means it’s in use. A [node pool of their own](/next/guides/node-pools/) lets pausing non-prod empty whole nodes. 4. **Spare capacity on purpose?** A node group minimum above use, long `scale-down-unneeded-time` or `scale-down-delay-after-add`, a `scale-down-utilization-threshold` under the nodes’ use, long `consolidateAfter`, tight Karpenter disruption `budgets`, or over-provisioning placeholder pods. Saved catches up as those nodes go ([Node pools for real savings](/next/guides/node-pools/)). By `pool`, `napwake_capacity_spare_cpu_cores` against `napwake_capacity_paused_cpu_cores` shows which pools still fit what’s paused ([Metrics](/next/reference/metrics/#savings)). ### Saved reads `unknown` `savedThisMonth`, `projectedMonthlySavings` and SAVED in `kubectl napwake status` read `unknown`, with why above the status table and in: 
```bash
kubectl get managedworkload my-api -n staging -o jsonpath='{.status.cost.savingsUnknown}'
```
 Under `watchNamespaces` the operator can’t see other namespaces’ pods, so it can’t tell what’s left on each node for paused replicas. It can’t measure saved, and exports no `napwake_capacity_*` or `napwake_savings_realised_dollars_per_hour` series. Freed is still measured. For saved, install without `watchNamespaces`, which watches pods cluster-wide, caching only each bound pod’s requests ([Data and access](/next/reference/data-and-access/)). ## ManagedWorkloads ### Target not found `TargetAvailable=False`, reason `TargetNotFound`, with a warning event of that name: 
```bash
kubectl get managedworkload my-api -n staging -o jsonpath='{.status.conditions}'
```
 Check the target exists (`kubectl get deployment my-api -n staging`), `target.kind` matches (Deployment or StatefulSet), and it shares the ManagedWorkload’s namespace. `spec.target` can’t change, so create another ManagedWorkload for another workload. Reason `TargetIgnored` means it’s labelled `napwake.dev/ignore`. ### Two ManagedWorkloads target one workload The oldest wins. The others report `DuplicateTarget=True`, naming it, and do nothing: 
```bash
kubectl get managedworkloads -n staging -o jsonpath='{range .items[*]}{.metadata.name}: {.spec.target.name}{"\n"}{end}'
```
 ### A `ManagedByAnotherInstall` warning Two installs watch its namespace, and the other, in the namespace the event names, took this ManagedWorkload first. Give each install its own `watchNamespaces` ([More than one install](/next/getting-started/installation/#more-than-one-install)). If the other is gone, `kubectl annotate managedworkload <name> -n <namespace> napwake.dev/operator-` hands it over. ### A paused workload’s ManagedWorkload lost its status It happens to one restored from a backup without status, or deleted without its finalizer and recreated. A `PauseRecovered` event means Napwake took the pause up again from the workload’s `napwake.dev/paused-from` ([how](/next/concepts/lifecycle/#idempotency)). It’s `Paused` again if still at zero, and wakes as usual. Leave that annotation alone. Napwake removes it at hand-back. A `PauseRecordLost` warning means there was no record, or the annotation couldn’t be read, as the message says. With no record anywhere, nothing was scaled, and the workload is `Running` again. If its replicas are at zero anyway, Napwake takes it for scaled to zero outside it and leaves it, so scale it up yourself. ### A ManagedWorkload let go of its workload without handing it back A `HandBackAbandoned` warning event means its namespace is being deleted, and handing the paused workload back failed for 5 minutes, such as when a KEDA webhook that’s down refuses to release its ScaledObject. It lets go rather than block the deletion, and the workload goes with the namespace. The message has the error. ## Installing and removing ### `helm uninstall` fails with `job napwake-uninstall-guard failed` Or, in Helm 4, `resource Job/napwake-system/napwake-uninstall-guard not ready`. ManagedWorkloads are left, whose workloads would stay paused. Helm 3.18 and later list them after `Napwake refuses to uninstall:`. Before 3.18, or in any Helm after a `helm rollback` with one, the guard’s log does: 
```bash
kubectl logs job/napwake-uninstall-guard -n napwake-system
```
 Remove the `napwake.dev/managed` label from those workloads and their namespaces, delete the ManagedWorkloads you wrote, and uninstall again. To keep Napwake, `helm rollback napwake -n napwake-system` marks the release deployed again, and until then `helm upgrade` fails with `has no deployed releases`. One still listed as being deleted is one the operator didn’t finish, usually as it isn’t running. Uninstall with `--no-hooks`, and restore the workloads by hand. With no CRD (`crds.install: false`), nothing is guarded. Argo CD 3.3 and later refuses to delete the Application the same way ([When `helm uninstall` refuses](/next/getting-started/installation/#when-helm-uninstall-refuses)). ### `helm upgrade` fails with `job napwake-upgrade-guard failed` Or, in Helm 4, `resource Job/napwake-system/napwake-upgrade-guard not ready`. The new `watchNamespaces` leaves out namespaces where workloads are managed, stranding them paused. Helm 3.18 and later list them after `Napwake refuses this upgrade:`, or the guard’s log does: 
```bash
kubectl logs job/napwake-upgrade-guard -n napwake-system
```
 Keep those namespaces, or stop managing their workloads first ([Narrowing watchNamespaces](/next/operations/upgrading/#narrowing-watchnamespaces)). Under Argo CD it fails the sync, in the PreSync hook. ### Workloads stay at zero after the operator was removed The operator or its CRD went while ManagedWorkloads were left. Nothing scales their workloads up, their Services still point at the doorman’s deleted pods, and deleting the ManagedWorkloads or CRD hangs in `Terminating` on the `napwake.dev/cleanup` finalizer. Reinstall and stop managing them, or [restore them by hand](/next/getting-started/installation/#restoring-workloads-after-the-operator-is-gone). ## None of these ### Debug checklist Put your install’s namespace for `napwake-system`. | Check | Command | | ---------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | Operator running | `kubectl get pods -n napwake-system` | | CRDs installed | `kubectl get crd managedworkloads.napwake.dev` | | Metrics server running | `kubectl top nodes` | | RBAC correct | `kubectl auth can-i get pods --as=system:serviceaccount:napwake-system:napwake`. The ServiceAccount is `napwake` for a Helm release named `napwake`, and `napwake-controller-manager` with `install.yaml` or the Kustomize manifests | | Events | `kubectl describe managedworkload <name> -n <ns>` | | Logs | `kubectl logs -n napwake-system -l control-plane=controller-manager` | | Status | `kubectl get managedworkload <name> -n <ns> -o yaml` | ### Getting help Not on this page? Check the [GitHub issues](https://github.com/napwake/napwake/issues) for known problems, then open one with what’s below. * The operator’s version (`kubectl get deploy -n napwake-system -o wide` shows the image), the plugin’s (`kubectl napwake version`), and Kubernetes’ * The ManagedWorkload’s YAML, sanitized * The relevant operator logs * The output of `kubectl describe managedworkload`

# Upgrading

> Upgrade Napwake, keep your Helm settings across upgrades, narrow watchNamespaces safely, and roll back.

Napwake upgrades in place from one Napwake release to the next. On 0.1.x or a 0.2.0 release candidate, [move to Napwake](#moving-to-napwake) instead. ## Moving to Napwake Hybernate was this project’s name before 0.2.0. There’s no upgrade path from it. Uninstall Hybernate, then install Napwake fresh, and opt your workloads in again with the `napwake.dev/` labels and annotations. Uninstalling a 0.2.0 release candidate as its docs say hands paused workloads back. On 0.1.x, delete its ManagedWorkloads, then scale the paused workloads back up by hand. 0.1.x leaves them at zero. The [changelog](https://github.com/napwake/napwake/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md) lists every name that changed. ### Wake on request, from before 0.2.0 Before 0.2.0, `spec.wake.onRequest: false`, or `hybernate.io/wake-on-request: "false"` with the label, turned waking on request off for one workload. That setting is gone. Napwake ignores the annotation, so the workload wakes on request. A manifest that still sets `spec.wake.onRequest` is refused: | Applied with | The error | | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `kubectl apply`, which checks fields strictly by default | `strict decoding error: unknown field "spec.wake.onRequest"` | | A server-side apply, as Flux’s is, or Argo CD’s with `ServerSideApply=true` | `.spec.wake.onRequest: field not declared in schema`, so the sync fails | So take `wake.onRequest` out of the ManagedWorkloads in your manifests in Git. Where it was `true`, remove it, since `spec.wake.mode: Request` is the default. Where it was `false`, put in what fits below, usually `mode: Ask`: 
```yaml
spec:
  wake:
    mode: Ask   # in place of onRequest: false
```
 | What turning it off was for | Set instead | | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | A workload on a public URL, which crawlers and scanners would keep awake, as it usually was | `spec.wake.mode: Ask`, or `napwake.dev/wake: "ask"` with the label. Only a person’s click wakes it, and anything else gets a `503` ([Asking before waking](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#asking-before-waking)) | | One port of a Service | List it in `napwake.dev/doorman-ignore-ports` on the Service. It fails while the workload is paused, as before | | The whole cluster | The Helm value `doorman.enabled: false`, as before | The `WakeOnRequest` reason `TurnedOff` is gone with it. ## Upgrade 1. Read the [changelog](https://github.com/napwake/napwake/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md) for the target version. Look for breaking changes, CRD schema changes and required steps. 2. Upgrade. The CRD is upgraded with the operator. * Helm With the values file you install with: 
     ```bash
     helm upgrade napwake oci://ghcr.io/napwake/charts/napwake \
       --version X.Y.Z \
       --namespace napwake-system \
       -f napwake-values.yaml
     ```
 | Upgrading with Helm | | | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Values | Give the file every time, or none. `--set` alone drops the values set before ([Keeping your settings](#keeping-your-settings)) | | The chart | Published only as an OCI artifact, so there’s no `helm repo` to update. Its version has no `v` | | The CRD | A template in the chart, so `helm upgrade` upgrades it too. Not if you installed with `crds.install: false` and manage it yourself | * kubectl 
     ```bash
     kubectl apply -f https://github.com/napwake/napwake/releases/download/vX.Y.Z/install.yaml
     ```
 * Kustomize Change each Napwake base’s `ref` in your kustomization to `vX.Y.Z`, then: 
     ```bash
     kubectl apply -k .
     ```
 * Argo CD or Flux Change the Application’s `targetRevision`, or the `OCIRepository`’s `tag`, to `X.Y.Z` in Git, and let it sync. To hand a `helm install` to either, see [Moving a Helm install to Argo CD or Flux](/next/getting-started/installation/#moving-a-helm-install-to-argo-cd-or-flux). 3. Check the operator: 
   ```bash
   kubectl get pods -n napwake-system
   kubectl logs -n napwake-system -l control-plane=controller-manager --tail=20
   ```
 4. Check the ManagedWorkloads are reconciling: 
   ```bash
   kubectl get managedworkloads --all-namespaces
   ```
 ## Keeping your settings Keep every Helm value you set in one file, and give it to `helm install` and every `helm upgrade`. Helm reuses a release’s values only on an upgrade given none. Given any, by `--set` or `-f`, it starts from the chart’s defaults and adds only those. Helm 3 and 4 do the same. | Installed with | Upgraded with | Keeps | | ------------------------------- | --------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | | `timezone` and `prometheus.url` | `--set timezone=America/New_York` | The time zone. It drops `prometheus.url` | | Whatever that left | The next `--set` | Only what that one names | Change a setting by changing the file. It holds all your settings in one place to review, the same in every Helm, ready for Git. Argo CD and Flux take the same values, so [moving to one](/next/getting-started/installation/#moving-a-helm-install-to-argo-cd-or-flux) is a copy. If you installed with `--set`, save the release’s values first. A release with none writes `null`, so put your settings there. 
```bash
helm get values napwake -n napwake-system -o yaml > napwake-values.yaml
```
 To change a setting, such as the [time zone](/next/getting-started/installation/#time-zone), edit the file and upgrade with it. Stay at the version in the `CHART` column of `helm list -n napwake-system`, so Napwake isn’t upgraded too. 
```bash
helm upgrade napwake oci://ghcr.io/napwake/charts/napwake \
  --version X.Y.Z \
  --namespace napwake-system \
  -f napwake-values.yaml
```
 ## Narrowing watchNamespaces `helm upgrade` stops with `job napwake-upgrade-guard failed`, or with Helm 4 `resource Job/napwake-system/napwake-upgrade-guard not ready`. Stop managing a namespace’s workloads before you drop it from `watchNamespaces`. Dropping one, or setting `watchNamespaces` where it was empty, removes the operator’s and the doorman’s Roles there. They stop watching it. Workloads paused there would stay at zero, their Services routed to doorman ports nothing serves. Their ManagedWorkloads’ finalizers would never run. So the chart’s upgrade guard refuses. While the operator still watches them, so it scales each one back up: 1. Remove the `napwake.dev/managed` label from the workloads and namespaces there. 2. Delete the ManagedWorkloads you wrote yourself there: 
   ```bash
   kubectl delete managedworkloads --all -n preview-1
   ```
 3. Upgrade. ## CRD compatibility | Change | Compatibility | | ------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------- | | Any, in `v1alpha1` | May break between minor versions. Always read the release notes | | A new field | Not breaking. New fields are optional, with defaults | | A field removed, or its type changed | Breaking, and called out in the release notes | The CRD is kept when the chart is uninstalled, so uninstalling and reinstalling never deletes your ManagedWorkloads. ## Forecast engine state Each ManagedWorkload keeps its forecast’s state in `status.prediction.state`. On upgrade: | State | What happens | | ----------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | A compatible version | Imported | | Saved by an older version | Discarded, with a `ForecastReset` event. The engine learns again, from the workload’s [CPU history](/next/concepts/forecasting/#learning-from-history), read again, when `prometheus.url` is set, otherwise from scratch | | Unreadable otherwise, such as after a hand edit | Discarded the same way, with a `ForecastReset` warning | ## Rollback * Helm 
  ```bash
  helm rollback napwake -n napwake-system
  ```
 * kubectl 
  ```bash
  kubectl apply -f https://github.com/napwake/napwake/releases/download/vPREVIOUS/install.yaml
  ```
 * Kustomize, Argo CD or Flux Put the previous release’s `ref`, `targetRevision` or `tag` back, and apply or sync it. Paused workloads stay paused during a rollback, and the previous operator version goes on managing them. Rolling back across a breaking API change isn’t supported. ## Version history Every change is on the [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/napwake/napwake/releases) page and in the [changelog](https://github.com/napwake/napwake/blob/main/CHANGELOG.md). Next * [Installation →Helm, Kustomize, Argo CD or Flux.](/next/getting-started/installation/) * [Helm values →Every value of the chart.](/next/reference/helm-values/)

# API reference

> Every field of the ManagedWorkload resource: its type, its default and what it does.

* Group `napwake.dev` * Version `v1alpha1` * Kind `ManagedWorkload` * Scope Namespaced [Spec 7](#spec-managedworkloadspec) * [`target`](#spec-target) * [`idlePolicy`](#spec-idlepolicy) * [`dependsOn`](#spec-dependson) * [`wake`](#spec-wake) * [`prediction`](#spec-prediction) * [`costTracking`](#spec-costtracking) * [`dryRun`](#spec-dryrun) [Status 17](#status-managedworkloadstatus) * [`phase`](#status-phase) * [`conditions`](#status-conditions) * [`lastActedAt`](#status-lastactedat) * [`lastPauseRequest`](#status-lastpauserequest) * [`lastWakeRequest`](#status-lastwakerequest) * [`turnedAway`](#status-turnedaway) * [`lastTransitionTime`](#status-lasttransitiontime) * [`activity`](#activity) * [`pause`](#status-pause) * [`learnedDependencies`](#status-learneddependencies) * [`lastScaledUp`](#status-lastscaledup) * [`doorman`](#status-doorman) * [`doormanDraining`](#status-doormandraining) * [`idleWait`](#status-idlewait) * [`dryRun`](#status-dryrun) * [`prediction`](#prediction) * [`cost`](#status-cost) [Types and values 3](#costrates) * [`CostRates`](#costrates) * [`ResourceSnapshot`](#status-pause-resources) * [`WorkloadPhase`](#workloadphase-values) ## Columns What `kubectl get managedworkloads` shows. | Column | Shows | | ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------- | | `Phase` | The phase | | `Freed` | What pausing freed this month | | `Saved` | What of that it saved | | `Would Free` | What dry-run measured it would free. Blank outside dry-run | | `Age` | Age | ## Spec (`ManagedWorkloadSpec`) | Field | Type | Default | Description | | ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `target` | `WorkloadRef` | required | The workload. Can’t be changed once set | | `target.kind` | `Deployment` or `StatefulSet` | `Deployment` | Its kind | | `target.name` | string | required | Its name, same namespace | | `idlePolicy` | `IdlePolicySpec` | | Idle detection. Without it, never paused automatically | | `idlePolicy.idleAfter` | duration, at least `1m` | `1h` | Time without activity before pausing | | `idlePolicy.activity.cpuThreshold` | int or string, 0.1-100, to two places | `10` | CPU % of requests above which it’s active. A decimal is written as a string, such as `"0.5"` | | `idlePolicy.activity.prometheus[].promQL` | string | required in each entry | PromQL instant query. Above zero is activity. At most 10 | | `idlePolicy.autoResume` | bool | `false` | Wake ahead of confident forecast demand | | `idlePolicy.pauseEarly` | bool | `false` | Pause after 20 idle minutes, before `idleAfter`, if a confident forecast expects quiet until then and the doorman would wake it. See [Pausing early](/next/concepts/forecasting/#pausing-early) | | `dependsOn[]` | `DependencyRef` | | Workloads this one needs. At most 20 | | `dependsOn[].namespace` | string | this namespace | Its namespace | | `dependsOn[].kind` | `Deployment` or `StatefulSet` | required | Its kind | | `dependsOn[].name` | string | required | Its name | | `dependsOn[].waitForReady` | bool | `false` | Hold this one’s resume until it’s Ready | | `wake` | `WakeSpec` | | What wakes it through the doorman while paused | | `wake.mode` | `Request` or `Ask` | `Request` | What wakes it through the doorman. `Request` is any request but a known probe’s. `Ask` is only a person’s click on **Wake it**, and anything else gets a `503`. See [Asking before waking](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#asking-before-waking) | | `wake.maxWait` | duration, at most `1h` | `2m` | How long a request is held while it wakes | | `wake.page` | bool | `true` | Answer a browser’s page load with the waking-up page, instead of holding it. With `mode: Ask`, a browser always gets a page | | `prediction` | `PredictionSpec` | `{}` | The forecast. Left out or `{}`, it takes the defaults | | `prediction.mode` | `eager`, `balanced` or `cautious` | `balanced` | How soon the forecast acts, at confidence 60, 75 or 85. See [Forecast setting](/next/concepts/forecasting/#forecast-setting) | | `prediction.confidence` | int, 50-100 | the mode’s | Accuracy (1 − WAPE, %) a season’s forecasts must reach to drive decisions. Overrides the mode’s | | `costTracking` | `CostTrackingSpec` | | Your own cost rates | | `costTracking.rates` | [`CostRates`](#costrates) | node list prices, then AWS defaults | Custom rates, none negative | | `dryRun` | bool | `false` | Measure without pausing. Turning it on wakes a paused workload. See [Dry run](/next/guides/dry-run/) | ### Durations | Duration | | | --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | | `45s`, `90m`, `1h30m` | Go durations above zero work | | `1d`, `2w`, spaces | Days, weeks and spaces don’t. Write `24h` or `168h` | | Anything else | The API server rejects it | ### CostRates For `costTracking.rates` and `cost.listRates`. | Field | Type | Default | Description | | ----------------- | -------- | ------- | ----------------- | | `cpuPerHour` | quantity | `0.031` | USD per vCPU-hour | | `memoryPerHour` | quantity | `0.004` | USD per GiB-hour | | `storagePerMonth` | quantity | `0.08` | USD per GiB-month | ## Status (`ManagedWorkloadStatus`) | Field | Type | Description | | --------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `phase` | [`WorkloadPhase`](#workloadphase-values) | Current lifecycle phase | | `conditions[]` | `Condition` | Standard Kubernetes conditions. See [Conditions and events](/next/reference/conditions-and-events/#status-conditions) | | `lastActedAt` | time | Last pause or wake, by Napwake or a scale-up from zero outside it | | `lastPauseRequest` | string | Last `napwake.dev/pause-requested` value acted on, so each acts once. Outcome in the `PauseRequest` condition. See [Pause now](/next/guides/pause/#pause-now) | | `lastWakeRequest` | string | Last `napwake.dev/wake-requested` value acted on, so each acts once. Outcome in the `WakeRequest` condition. See [Wake now](/next/guides/pause/#wake-now) | | `turnedAway` | `TurnedAway` | With `wake.mode: Ask`, what the doorman turned away during the current pause, or the last once it’s awake. `pausedAt`, when that pause began, and `requests`. Brought up to date about once a minute. See [What it turns away](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#what-it-turns-away) | | `lastTransitionTime` | time | Last phase change | | `learnedDependencies` | `LearnedDependencies` | Found by Napwake, held and woken like `dependsOn`. Fields `from`, `at` and `dependencies`, each with `namespace`, `kind`, `name` and `source`, which is `environment` (with `via`, `address`) or `wake` (with `lastSeen`). See [Learned dependencies](/next/concepts/dependencies/#learned-dependencies) | | `lastScaledUp` | `ScaledUp` | Last scale-up outside Napwake while paused, which wakes it. Fields `at`, `replicas`, `by` (field manager, such as `argocd-controller`, or `unknown` through the scale subresource, as `kubectl scale`, which records none) and `gitOps` (`Argo CD`, `Flux`, `Fleet`, `kapp` or `Kluctl`, if from Git). See [GitOps tools](/next/guides/gitops/) | | `doorman[]` | `DoormanRoute` | Service ports routed to the doorman while paused, with `service`, `portName`, `doormanPort` and `forwarded` (behind GKE NEGs, through the [forwarder](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#gke-container-native-load-balancing-negs)) | | `doormanDraining[]` | `DrainingPort` | Doorman ports that routed it until it woke, with `port` and `until`. No other workload gets the port before `until`, so a late client never reaches another. Kept in status, so an operator that restarts or becomes leader honours it | ### `activity` | Field | Type | Description | | ----------------------------- | --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `activity.lastActivityTime` | time | Latest activity, any source | | `activity.lastActivitySource` | string | `created`, `woke`, `request`, `cpu`, `rollout`, `annotation`, `prometheus`, `unobserved` or `scaled-up` | | `activity.pauseAt` | time | When it pauses without more activity | | `activity.lastEvaluatedTime` | time | Last activity check | | `activity.templateHash` | string | Pod template fingerprint, to detect deploys | | `activity.heldByDependents` | \[]string | Awake dependents keeping it from pausing (`HeldByDependents`), as `namespace/name`. At most 10, first by name | | `activity.forecastBusyFrom` | time | Start of the hour of expected demand, while the forecast holds off the pause (`IdleVetoed`) | | `activity.forecastQuietUntil` | time | End of the quiet hours expected when the forecast brought the pause forward (`PausedEarly`). Kept until the pause, or dry-run’s would-be pause, ends | ### `pause` | Field | Type | Description | | --------------------------------------- | ------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `pause` | `PauseStatus` | What a pause changed, from before scaling to zero until running again | | `pause.previousReplicas` | int32 | Replicas before the pause, restored on resume | | `pause.startedAt` | time | When the pause began, as it went `Pausing`, before anything was scaled. Or when Napwake took up a pause whose record was lost. The asleep page’s **Wake it** button is good for this pause alone. See [The Wake it button](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#the-wake-it-button) | | `pause.routesWrittenAt` | time | When the doorman’s routes for this pause were stored in status, which every doorman pod listens on. Its EndpointSlices go in 5 seconds after. Unset for a workload the doorman doesn’t route | | `pause.routedAt` | time | When the doorman’s EndpointSlices first stood in front of its Services for this pause, beside its pods. It’s scaled to zero a few seconds after. Unset for a workload the doorman doesn’t route. See [What happens in a pause](/next/guides/pause/#what-happens-in-a-pause) | | `pause.requested` | bool | The pause was asked for, with `napwake.dev/pause-requested`, not decided by the idle clock or the forecast | | `pause.cpuBefore` `pause.cpuJustBefore` | string | The most CPU it used, as a percentage of its requests. `cpuBefore` is through the stretch without activity that ran its clock out. `cpuJustBefore` is in its last 15 minutes, or `idleAfter` if shorter. Unset when none was measured. See [Paused while in use](/next/concepts/idle-detection/#paused-while-in-use) | | `pause.pausedAt` | time | When scaled to zero, unset before. `Paused` once its pods stop | | `pause.resumedAt` | time | When the current resume scaled it up. Scaled to zero after that, not by Napwake, it’s left off | | `pause.firstReadyAt` | time | When the current resume first saw a replica Ready. The rest get 2 minutes more | | `pause.scaledObject` | string | KEDA ScaledObject held at zero while paused. See [HPA and KEDA](/next/guides/autoscalers/) | | `pause.scaledObjectPausedReplicas` | string | Its own `autoscaling.keda.sh/paused-replicas` before the pause, put back after | | `pause.resources` | `ResourceSnapshot` | What it ran as the pause began, the base for savings. `replicas`, per replica `cpuMillis`, `memoryBytes`, claims’ `storageBytes`, and `nodePools` (`name`, `""` without a pool label, and its `pods`) | | `pause.wakeAnnotations` | object | Activity annotations on the ManagedWorkload (`workload`) and target (`target`) as the pause began. A change wakes it | | `pause.queryActiveSincePause` | bool | A [Prometheus activity query](/next/guides/prometheus-signals/#while-the-workload-is-paused) has been active at every check since a requested pause began. They don’t wake it until a check finds all quiet | ### `idleWait` | Field | Type | Description | | --------------------------------------- | ---------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `idleWait` | `IdleWaitStatus` | Pauses made while it was in use, which lengthen its idle clock, and its CPU use idle. See [Paused while in use](/next/concepts/idle-detection/#paused-while-in-use) | | `idleWait.wrongPauses` | int32 | Pauses in a row its own use undid soon after they began. Each doubles how long the idle clock waits, up to `idleAfter` plus an hour. A pause that holds sets it back to zero | | `idleWait.lastWrongPause` | `WrongPause` | The latest. `pausedAt`, `after` (how soon it was undone), `by` (`request`, `click`, `wakeRequest` or `annotation`), `from` (the caller’s address, for a request), and `inUseCPU` | | `idleWait.idleCPU` `idleWait.idleCPUAt` | string, time | The most CPU it used, as a percentage of its requests, before its pauses that held, over the last week. `idleCPUAt` is when it was last measured | ### `dryRun` | Field | Type | Description | | ------------------------------------------------ | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `dryRun` | `DryRunStatus` | What dry-run measured. Only while `spec.dryRun` is true | | `dryRun.since` | time | When measuring started | | `dryRun.pauses` | int32 | Would-be pauses, including one under way | | `dryRun.slept` | duration | Total length of finished would-be pauses | | `dryRun.freedCPUHours` `dryRun.freedMemoryHours` | quantity | vCPU-hours and GiB-hours requested during `slept` | | `dryRun.estimatedFreed` | string | Those hours priced, for display, counting a would-be pause under way to the last status write. What pausing would have freed. The `Would Free` column | | `dryRun.resources` | `ResourceSnapshot` | What it ran as the current would-be pause began | | `dryRun.queryActiveSincePause` | bool | As [`pause.queryActiveSincePause`](#status-pause-queryactivesincepause), since the would-be pause under way was asked for. Queries don’t end it until all are quiet | ### `prediction` | Field | Type | Description | | ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `prediction.dailyPhase` `prediction.weeklyPhase` | string | Each season’s phase, `Observing`, `Suggesting` or `Active` | | `prediction.dailyConfidence` `prediction.weeklyConfidence` | int | Accuracy % over the last 24 and 168 observed hours | | `prediction.timeZone` | string | IANA zone for hours and weekdays, such as `Europe/London`. The operator’s `--timezone` (Helm value `timezone`) | | `prediction.threshold` | int | Confidence it acts at, `spec.prediction.confidence` or the mode’s | | `prediction.acting` | bool | Whether it drives decisions, holding off pauses and, with `autoResume`, waking ahead of demand | | `prediction.startsActing` | time | When it should start acting, if the coming days repeat the last week. Unset while acting, or if not within four weeks | | `prediction.reason` `prediction.message` | string | Why it is or isn’t acting, as `Acting`, `Observing`, `EarningConfidence`, `Relearning` or `NotLearning`, and a sentence. See [When it acts](/next/concepts/forecasting/#when-it-acts) | | `prediction.history` | `PredictionHistory` | What it learned from Prometheus history when management began. Fields `reason` (`Learned`, `NoPrometheusURL`, `NoHistory`, `NoCPURequests`, `QueryFailed`), `hours`, `from`, `to`, `message`. See [Learning from history](/next/concepts/forecasting/#learning-from-history) | | `prediction.state` | string | Learned state, gzipped and base64-encoded | ### `cost` | Field | Type | Description | | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `cost` | `CostStatus` | This calendar month’s cost, in UTC. See [Cost tracking](/next/concepts/cost-tracking/#status-fields) | | `cost.tracked` | duration | How much of the month the totals cover | | `cost.lastAccumulatedAt` | time | When totals were last brought up to date | | `cost.awakeCPUHours` `cost.awakeMemoryHours` | quantity | vCPU-hours and GiB-hours requested while awake | | `cost.storageHours` | quantity | GiB-hours of claims its pods mount, in every phase | | `cost.pausedCPUHours` `cost.pausedMemoryHours` | quantity | vCPU-hours and GiB-hours the paused replicas had requested, while paused. What pausing freed | | `cost.savedCPUHours` `cost.savedMemoryHours` | quantity | Its share of the paused requests its node pools’ spare capacity can’t hold, every 5 minutes. What pausing saved, at least. See [How saved is measured](/next/concepts/cost-tracking/#how-saved-is-measured) | | `cost.costThisMonth` | string | Awake and storage hours, priced | | `cost.freedThisMonth` | string | Paused hours, priced | | `cost.savedThisMonth` | string | Saved hours, priced. `unknown` while `savingsUnknown` is set | | `cost.savingsUnknown` | string | Why saved can’t be measured, such as pods outside `watchNamespaces` being invisible. Unset when measured | | `cost.costWithoutNapwakeThisMonth` | string | `costThisMonth` plus `freedThisMonth` | | `cost.projectedMonthlyCost` `cost.projectedMonthlyFreed` `cost.projectedMonthlySavings` | string | The month so far, carried to a full month. `pending` until a day is tracked | | `cost.running` | `ResourceSnapshot` | What it ran when last brought up to date while awake | | `cost.pricedAt` `cost.pricedTemplateHash` | time, string | When requests and list rates were last read, and the pod template then | | `cost.listRates` | [`CostRates`](#costrates) | List rates of the nodes its pods last ran on, spot at spot. Unset without a list price | | `cost.listPrice` | string | Which list prices `listRates` are, `OnDemand`, `Spot` or `SpotAndOnDemand`. Unset with it | | `cost.resourceReduction` | `ResourceReduction` | What the current pause freed, as `cpuMillis`, `memoryBytes` and `replicas`. Only while paused | ## WorkloadPhase values | Value | Meaning | | ---------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `Running` `Idle` `Pausing` `Paused` `Resuming` | The phases. [Lifecycle](/next/concepts/lifecycle/) says what each means | | No phase | Until the first reconcile | | `Creating` | Accepted by the schema but never set | Next * [ManagedWorkload guide →Writing one yourself.](/next/guides/managed-workload/) * [Lifecycle →Phases, conditions, events.](/next/concepts/lifecycle/) * [Labels and annotations →Settings as annotations.](/next/reference/labels-annotations/)
