Status conditions Each has a `Reason`, a `Message` and a `LastTransitionTime`. A condition appears once it first applies. After that it shows `False` with a False reason below, rather than going. Only `GitOpsConflict` and `Autoscaled` are removed. | Type | `True` means | Reasons | | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `TargetAvailable` | The target exists, and is managed | `TargetExists`. False: `TargetNotFound`, `TargetIgnored` (labelled `napwake.dev/ignore`) | | `MetricsAvailable` | CPU can be measured, for the activity clock and forecast | `MetricsReported`. Unknown: `NoReadyPods`, `AwaitingPodMetrics`, while new pods have no metrics. False: `NoPodMetrics`, `NoCPURequests`, `MetricsUnavailable` | | `PrometheusAvailable` | The activity queries answer. Only set when there are some | `QueriesEvaluated`. False: `QueryFailed`, `EndpointNotConfigured` | | `PrometheusQueryEmpty` | A query has matched no series since first evaluated, usually from a misspelt metric or label. It doesn’t stop a pause | `NoSeries`. False: `QueriesMatchSeries` | | `IdleVetoed` | Idle, but a confident forecast expects demand in the coming hour, which the message names. The pause waits | `ForecastExpectsDemand`. False: `NotVetoed` | | `WakeOnRequest` | Its Services are routed to the doorman while paused, so a request wakes it, or with [`wake.mode: Ask`](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#asking-before-waking) a person’s click. With `Ask`, its message says the doorman asks a person’s browser first, and turns away other requests. A Services problem stays once it’s awake, the message starting `at its last pause:` | `DoormanRouted`. False: [why it isn’t routed](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#when-a-workload-isnt-routed) (`NoServices`, `ServedByOtherPods`, `UnsupportedIPFamily`, `UnsupportedLoadBalancer`, `ForwarderNotReady`, `DoormanUnavailable`, `NoFreePort`, `TooManyPorts`, `RoutingFailed`, `DoormanDisabled`, `NodeLocalTraffic`). `NotPaused`, `TargetNotFound`, `DuplicateTarget` once routing is removed | | `HeldByDependents` | Kept awake for its dependents | `DependentsAwake`. False: `NoAwakeDependents`, or `NotIdle` once its own activity restarts its clock | | `WaitingForDependencies` | Its resume waits for a `waitForReady` dependency to be Ready | `DependencyNotReady`. False: `DependenciesReady`. Also `DependencyWontStart`, naming a dependency it doesn’t wait for, as it can’t start or wasn’t Ready in time. Or `DependencyCycle`, naming one that waits for it in turn | | `DependencyCycle` | `dependsOn` forms a cycle through it, so it won’t pause | `DependencyCycle`. False: `NoCycle` | | `DependencyNotFound` | A `dependsOn` workload doesn’t exist, or can’t be seen | `DependencyNotFound`, or `DependencyNotVisible` when it’s only outside `watchNamespaces` or not readable. False: `DependenciesFound`, `NoDependencies` | | `DependencyNotAllowed` | A dependency, declared or learned, is in a namespace whose `napwake.dev/dependents-from` doesn’t list this one. It isn’t held, woken or waited for | `DependencyNotAllowed`. False: `DependenciesAllowed`, `NoDependencies` | | `DuplicateTarget` | An older ManagedWorkload manages the target. This one hands back any pause it made, then does nothing | `DuplicateTarget`. False: `Resolved` | | `Protected` | It’s never paused. It’s in a protected namespace, Napwake’s own operator or doorman, or a StatefulSet that deletes its PVCs when scaled down | `ProtectedNamespace`, `NapwakeComponent`, `PVCsDeletedOnScaleDown`. False: `NotProtected` | | `PauseRequest` | The last [pause request](/next/guides/pause/#pause-now) paused it, or found it paused. False says why not. Until the next request, it says what happened and when, such as `paused as requested at 2026-10-21 03:32 UTC` | `Paused`, `AlreadyPaused`. False: `DryRun`, `ForecastExpectsDemand`, `ActiveUntil`, `HeldByDependents`, `DependencyCycle`, `Protected`, `ScaledToZero`, `TargetNotFound`, `TargetIgnored`, `DuplicateTarget`, `PredatesStatus`, `PauseCancelled` (a request came as it paused) | | `WakeRequest` | The last [wake request](/next/guides/pause/#wake-now) woke it, or found it awake. False says why not. Until the next request, it says what happened and when, such as `woken as requested at 2026-10-21 03:32 UTC` | `Woken`, `AlreadyAwake`. False: `TargetNotFound`, `TargetIgnored`, `DuplicateTarget`, `Protected`, `ScaledToZero`, `BeingDeleted`, `PredatesStatus` | | `GitOpsConflict` | A [GitOps tool](/next/guides/gitops/) undid its last pause. Removed once a pause holds for an hour | `PauseUndone` | | `ResumeIncomplete` | The last resume stopped waiting with replicas not Ready. It runs with the Ready ones until all are ([troubleshooting](/next/operations/troubleshooting/#workload-resumed-with-replicas-not-ready)) | `ReplicasNotReady`. False: `AllReplicasReady` | | `PauseIncomplete` | The last pause stopped waiting for pods still running a minute past their termination grace period. It’s `Paused` regardless ([troubleshooting](/next/operations/troubleshooting/#workload-paused-with-pods-still-running)) | `PodsStillRunning`. False: `PodsStopped`, `NotPaused` | | `PausedWhileInUse` | Its own use undid its last pause soon after it began. So its idle clock waits longer, until a pause holds. The message says what to change ([Paused while in use](/next/concepts/idle-detection/#paused-while-in-use)) | `WokenSoonAfterPause`, `PauseCancelled`. False: `PauseHeld` | | `Autoscaled` | An HPA or KEDA ScaledObject scales it, with its range. Removed when neither does ([HPA and KEDA](/next/guides/autoscalers/)) | `HPA`, `KEDA` | | `ScaledToZero` | Scaled to zero outside Napwake, so left off. Not paused, woken or routed until its replicas are above zero | `ScaledToZero`. False: `HasReplicas` | ## Events Each user-visible change emits one event, as it happens, shown by `kubectl describe`. The opt-in controller puts `Managed`, `NoLongerManaged`, `InvalidSetting`, `LabelIgnored` and `Protected` on the Deployment or StatefulSet itself. In dry-run, events for decisions it holds back start with `[dry-run]` ([Dry run](/next/guides/dry-run/#events)). Every event on the ManagedWorkload, by what it’s about. | About | Events | | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | The lifecycle | `IdleDetected`, `PausedEarly` (brought forward by [`pauseEarly`](/next/concepts/forecasting/#pausing-early)), `PauseRequested`, `Paused`, `Resumed`, `ActivityResumed` (in dry-run, with what the pause would have freed), `Unobserved` (a [gap in watching](/next/concepts/idle-detection/#restarts-and-outages) restarted the idle clock, and how long it was) | | Its pause record lost from status | `PauseRecovered` (from the target’s `napwake.dev/paused-from`). The warning `PauseRecordLost` (nothing to finish it from) | | A pause request not acted on | Its reason says why, as the `PauseRequest` condition’s does | | A wake request not acted on | Its reason says why, as the `WakeRequest` condition’s does | | What woke it | `WokeByActivity`, `WokenByRequest` (from the doorman, saying a person asked when it’s a click on **Wake it**), `WakeRequested` (from `napwake.dev/wake-requested`, as `kubectl napwake wake` sets it), `AutoResume`, `ScaledUp`, `DryRunWake`, and `PauseCancelled` (whatever would wake it came as it paused, so the pause was [called off](/next/guides/pause/#a-request-calls-the-pause-off)) | | A pause made while it was in use | The warning `PausedWhileInUse`, with what to change ([Paused while in use](/next/concepts/idle-detection/#paused-while-in-use)) | | Left off, scaled to zero outside Napwake | `ScaledToZero` | | What holds it awake | `IdleVetoed`, `HeldByDependents`, `DependencyCycle` | | A GitOps tool undoing a pause | `GitOpsConflict`, and `GitOpsConflictResolved` when that ends | | Dependencies | The warnings `DependencyWontStart` and `DependencyCycle` (a resume not waiting for a `waitForReady` dependency) and `DependencyWakeFailed` (one couldn’t be woken). `DependenciesLearned`, for learned ones | | The forecast | `PredictionFed` each hour it learns, `RegimeChange` when demoted, `ForecastReset` when its saved state can’t be read. When Napwake begins managing a workload, `ForecastLearnedHistory` if it learned the CPU history from Prometheus. `ForecastLearningLive` if there was none, or the warning `ForecastHistoryUnavailable` if it couldn’t be read | | The GKE NEG [forwarder](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#gke-container-native-load-balancing-negs) | `ForwarderStarted` beside a paused workload, `ForwarderStopped` once it’s removed after the wake | | Problems | `TargetNotFound`, `DuplicateTarget`, `NoPodMetrics`, `NoCPURequests`, `MetricsUnavailable`, `QueryFailed`, `EndpointNotConfigured`, `PrometheusQueryEmpty`, `InvalidActivityAnnotation`, `RoutingFailed`, `UnsupportedLoadBalancer`, `RequestNotServed`, `Protected`, and these: | | Problem | Means | | ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `NoReadinessProbe` | A routed workload’s pods have no readiness probe, so a waking request may reach the app before it can answer ([Readiness probes](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#readiness-probes)) | | `ResumeIncomplete` | In place of `Resumed`, when the resume stopped waiting for replicas not Ready. `AllReplicasReady` once they are | | `PauseIncomplete` | In place of `Paused`, when the pause stopped waiting for pods that didn’t stop | | `HandBackAbandoned` | A ManagedWorkload in a namespace being deleted let go of its workload | | `ManagedByAnotherInstall` | This install leaves the ManagedWorkload to the [one that took it on](/next/getting-started/installation/#more-than-one-install) | Next * [Lifecycle →The phases, and what moves a workload between them.](/next/concepts/lifecycle/) * [Troubleshooting →Common problems, and how to fix them.](/next/operations/troubleshooting/)

# Configuration reference

> The flags of the binary, /manager, which runs as the operator, the doorman or a forwarder, with their defaults, endpoints, leader election, TLS and logs.

The binary is `/manager`. The Helm chart sets its flags from its [values](/next/reference/helm-values/), so you need them only for your own manifests. [`/metrics`](#endpoint-metrics) ## Operator flags ### Metrics and probes | Flag | Default | Description | | ----------------------------- | -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--metrics-bind-address` | `0` (disabled) | Metrics address, `:8443` for HTTPS or `:8080` for HTTP. The chart and the Kustomize manifests use `:8443` | | `--metrics-secure` | `true` | HTTPS, only to callers allowed to `get` `/metrics` (TokenReview, SubjectAccessReview). A scraper needs such a token, which the chart’s `metrics.readerSubjects` binds ([Monitoring](/next/operations/monitoring/)). `false` is plain HTTP. See [TLS configuration](#tls-configuration) | | `--metrics-cert-path` | | Metrics TLS certificate directory. Without it, a self-signed one is generated | | `--metrics-cert-name` | `tls.crt` | Certificate file | | `--metrics-cert-key` | `tls.key` | Key file | | `--enable-http2` | `false` | Allow HTTP/2 for metrics | | `--health-probe-bind-address` | `:8081` | Probe address. See [Endpoints](#endpoints) | ### Operator | Flag | Default | Description | | ----------------------------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--leader-elect` | `false` | One replica reconciles. A leader that stops releases its lease, so the next, or a rollout’s new one, takes over at once. Ignored by the doorman, where every replica serves traffic. See [Leader election](#leader-election) | | `--max-concurrent-reconciles` | `8` | ManagedWorkloads reconciled at once, so a slow metrics or Prometheus query doesn’t hold up wakes, which queue ahead of periodic checks. At least 1 | | `--timezone` | `UTC` | IANA zone, such as `Europe/London`, for forecasts’ hours and weekdays, so they follow daylight saving. Zone database built in | | `--kubeconfig` | in-cluster | Kubeconfig path, outside the cluster only | ### Prometheus | Flag | Default | Description | | ----------------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--prometheus-url` | | Prometheus API base URL, `http` or `https` with a host, such as `http://prometheus.monitoring.svc:9090`. Needed for `idlePolicy.activity.prometheus`. Starts each new workload’s forecast from up to four weeks of CPU history. See [Learning from history](/next/concepts/forecasting/#learning-from-history) | | `--prometheus-selector` | | Matchers added to the operator’s own (history) query, such as `cluster="staging"`. Written as inside PromQL’s braces, with `=`, `!=`, `=~` or `!~` and a double-quoted or backquoted value, comma-separated. For a Prometheus with several clusters’ series, so another cluster’s same-named pods aren’t read as this one’s. Workloads’ activity queries run as written. Needs `--prometheus-url` | ### Namespaces | Flag | Default | Description | | ------------------------ | --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--watch-namespaces` | every namespace | Comma-separated namespaces to work in, a Role in each | | `--protected-namespaces` | | Comma-separated patterns, such as `prod-*`, of namespaces never managed, as if labelled `napwake.dev/protected=true`, unless labelled `napwake.dev/allow-protected=true`. `kube-*`, `$POD_NAMESPACE` and the doorman’s namespace are always protected. See [Protected namespaces](/next/guides/opt-in/#protected-namespaces) | ### Opt-in defaults For workloads opted in with the label, when neither they nor their namespace set it. | Flag | Default | Description | | ------------------------- | ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--default-idle-after` | `1h` | Idle time before pausing. At least `1m` | | `--default-cpu-threshold` | `10` | CPU % of requests that counts as active. 0.1 to 100, at most two decimal places | | `--default-dry-run` | `false` | Measure without pausing, unless `napwake.dev/dry-run` is set | | `--default-pause-early` | `false` | Let a confident forecast of quiet hours pause before `idleAfter` runs out, unless `napwake.dev/pause-early` is set. See [Pausing early](/next/concepts/forecasting/#pausing-early) | | `--default-forecast` | `balanced` | How soon the forecast acts, `eager`, `balanced` or `cautious`, unless `napwake.dev/forecast` is set | | `--default-wake` | `request` | What wakes a paused workload through the doorman, `request` or `ask`, unless `napwake.dev/wake` is set. See [Asking before waking](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#asking-before-waking) | ### Doorman | Flag | Default | Description | | ------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--doorman-service` | `napwake-doorman` | The doorman’s Service, where paused workloads are routed. Empty turns off wake on request | | `--doorman-ports-per-workload` | `32` | Most doorman ports per workload, one per routed Service port. Over it, it isn’t routed (`WakeOnRequest=False`, reason `TooManyPorts`) | | `--doorman-ports-per-namespace` | `256` | Most doorman ports per namespace, its workloads together | | `--doorman-namespace` | `$POD_NAMESPACE`, else `napwake-system` | The doorman Service’s namespace | | `--doorman-health-check-user-agents` | | Comma-separated `User-Agent` prefixes of health checkers and scrapers, besides the built-in ones, whose `GET` and `HEAD` don’t wake a workload. Helm value `doorman.healthCheckUserAgents` | | `--wake-key-secret` | | The doorman’s. A Secret in its namespace holding the key it signs the [Wake it button](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#the-wake-it-button) with, which it fills when empty. Every replica must share it, since a click can reach any of them. Without it, each signs with its own, which works only for one replica. The chart sets `<fullname>-doorman-wake-key` | ### GKE forwarder | Flag | Default | Description | | --------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--neg-forwarder` | `false` | Route Services behind GKE NEGs through a forwarder Deployment the operator runs in each paused workload’s namespace, instead of leaving them unrouted. Needs `create` and `delete` on Deployments. Helm value `doorman.negForwarder.enabled`. See [GKE container-native load balancing](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#gke-container-native-load-balancing-negs) | | `--neg-forwarder-image` | | The forwarders’ image, the operator’s own. Required with `--neg-forwarder` | | `--neg-forwarder-replicas` | `2` | Pods per forwarder. At least 1 | | `--neg-forwarder-resources` | `{"requests":{"cpu":"10m","memory":"32Mi"},"limits":{"memory":"64Mi"}}` | Each forwarder pod’s resources, as JSON | | `--neg-forwarder-doorman-service` | `napwake-doorman-pods` | Headless Service in `--doorman-namespace` selecting the doorman’s pods, whose DNS name forwarders resolve | ### Modes | Flag | Default | Description | | ------------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--doorman` | `false` | Run as the doorman instead of the operator. The doorman Deployment sets it | | `--forwarder` | `false` | Run as a [forwarder](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#gke-container-native-load-balancing-negs) instead of the operator. The operator’s forwarder Deployments set it, with `--forward` (routes, comma-separated `listen=doorman` port pairs) and `--forwarder-doorman` (the doorman pods’ DNS name) | | `--guard` | | Run as the chart’s `uninstall` or `upgrade` hook (`guard.uninstall`, `guard.upgrade`). Waits up to two and a half minutes for ManagedWorkloads being deleted, then exits 1, listing those left without an operator. `uninstall` counts every one in `--watch-namespaces` (all when empty) or with the `napwake.dev/cleanup` finalizer. `upgrade` counts those with the finalizer outside the new `--watch-namespaces` | ### Checked at startup The operator exits with an error naming the flag. | Flag | Refused when | | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | | `--protected-namespaces` | A pattern isn’t a valid glob | | `--prometheus-url` | Not `http` or `https` with a host | | `--prometheus-selector` | Not label matchers, or set without `--prometheus-url` | | `--default-idle-after` | Under `1m` | | `--default-cpu-threshold` | Outside 0.1 to 100, or more than two decimal places | | `--default-forecast` | Not `eager`, `balanced` or `cautious` | | `--default-wake` | Not `request` or `ask` | | `--max-concurrent-reconciles`, `--doorman-ports-per-workload`, `--doorman-ports-per-namespace` | Below 1 | | `--timezone` | Not a known IANA zone | ## Endpoints | Endpoint | Port | Answers | | ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `/healthz` | 8081 | Liveness. 200 while the process runs | | `/readyz` | 8081 | Readiness. For the doorman, 200 once its routes are loaded and it reached the API server in the last 30 seconds. Fails while it can’t listen on a routed port for over 30 seconds, and while shutting down | | `/readyz`, a forwarder’s | 8081, or the next port the workload doesn’t serve, for both its probes | 200 while it reaches the doorman on every port it passes connections to. Fails once it starts shutting down | | `/metrics` | 8443 in the chart | Prometheus metrics, HTTPS by default. See [Metrics](/next/reference/metrics/) | ## Leader election 
```plaintext
--leader-elect=true
```
 For HA, with more than one replica. The leader holds the Lease `479a98fc.napwake.dev` in the operator’s namespace. A standby takes over if it fails. ## TLS configuration 
```plaintext
--metrics-secure=false --metrics-bind-address=:8080
```
 Plain HTTP. Not for production. 
```plaintext
--metrics-cert-path=/certs/metrics
```
 Your own certificate, not a self-signed one. ## Logging | Flag | Default | Description | | ------------------------ | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `--zap-log-level` | `info` | `debug`, `info`, `error`, `panic`, or a whole number above 0 for more verbose debug levels | | `--zap-encoder` | `json` | `json`, or `console` for logs to read by eye (`--zap-encoder=console`, the chart’s `logEncoder: console`). The operator and the doorman log through `logr` (controller-runtime) | | `--zap-devel` | `false` | Development defaults, with console encoding, debug level and stack traces from warnings | | `--zap-stacktrace-level` | `error` | The level stack traces start at, `info`, `error` or `panic` | | `--zap-time-encoding` | RFC 3339 | `epoch`, `millis`, `nano`, `iso8601`, `rfc3339` or `rfc3339nano` | The key fields in log entries. | Field | What it holds | | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `name`, `namespace` | The object reconciled, such as the ManagedWorkload. controller-runtime adds them to all its entries, with `controller` and `reconcileID` | | `workload` | The workload or ManagedWorkload an entry names itself, with its `namespace` | | `from`, `to` | Phases of a `phase transition` entry | | `reason` | What caused a phase transition | | `phase` | Phase after a reconcile, on `reconciled` entries at debug level (`--zap-log-level=debug`) | | `error` | What went wrong, on failure entries | Next * [Helm values →The chart values that set these flags.](/next/reference/helm-values/) * [Metrics →What /metrics serves.](/next/reference/metrics/) * [Monitoring →Scraping and alerts.](/next/operations/monitoring/)

# Data and access

> Everything the Napwake operator and its doorman read, write, and send, for a security review.

* Never reads Your Secrets. The doorman reads one of its own * Deletes only Its own ManagedWorkloads, EndpointSlices and forwarder Deployments * Leaves the cluster Nothing, unless configured to * Telemetry None ## What it reads | Resource | Why | How | | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Deployments, StatefulSets | Replicas, pod template, labels and annotations of the workloads it manages | Watched | | Pods | Its running pods’ requests. CPU activity is judged against the app containers’, and all containers’, sidecars included, price it. Which pod sent a request that woke a workload. Requests of every pod bound to a node, to measure each node pool’s spare capacity, which [saved](/next/concepts/cost-tracking/#how-saved-is-measured) is worked out from. With `doorman.negForwarder.enabled`, forwarder pods’ addresses and readiness | Watched, caching only bound, unfinished pods, and only their name, node, phase and requests. The rest is read from the API server. App containers’ requests at most every 5 minutes. Not watched with `watchNamespaces` | | Pods, by the doorman | The pod each endpoint of a woken workload names, to check the address is that pod’s | Addresses kept 30 seconds | | Pod metrics (`metrics.k8s.io`) | CPU use, the activity clock’s main signal | When needed, not cached | | Services | Which to route a paused workload’s requests through, and which workload an address in an environment names | Watched | | Ingresses | Class and backend Services, to find those a GKE Ingress puts behind container-native load balancing, which can’t be routed to the doorman | Watched | | EndpointSlices | Its own, routing paused workloads to the doorman, and the doorman Service’s, to find the doorman’s pods | Watched | | EndpointSlices, by the doorman | All in the watched namespaces, to find a woken workload’s Ready pods | Watched | | ConfigMaps | Those a managed workload takes environment variables from, to learn its dependencies. `kube-system/cluster-autoscaler-status`, whose existence shows Cluster Autoscaler runs | One by one with `get`, not cached, never listed or written. Only the metadata of `cluster-autoscaler-status`, every 10 minutes | | PersistentVolumeClaims | Size, to price the storage a paused workload keeps | Watched | | HorizontalPodAutoscalers, KEDA ScaledObjects | What scales a workload, and its range | Watched | | Namespaces | Labels, for opt-in, settings and protection | Watched | | Nodes | Labels (instance type, region, spot, node pool), to price workloads and group them into pools. Allocatable CPU and memory, schedulable and Ready, to measure each pool’s spare capacity | Watched, keeping only those fields and dropping image lists and the rest. Metadata listed every 10 minutes, to tell which node autoscaler runs | | Karpenter NodePools (`karpenter.sh/v1`) | Names and `spec.disruption`, to report which node autoscaler runs | Listed every 10 minutes, not cached. Nothing without Karpenter installed | | Deployments in `kube-system` | Names, to find a Cluster Autoscaler that hasn’t written its status ConfigMap | Metadata listed every 10 minutes, only while that ConfigMap is missing | | ManagedWorkloads | Its own configuration | Watched | | Its wake key Secret, `<fullname>-doorman-wake-key`, by the doorman | The key it signs the asleep page’s [Wake it button](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#the-wake-it-button) with, shared by its replicas | Read by name every minute, from the API server, not cached. Written once, to fill it when it’s empty | | Any other Secret | **Never**, of any kind, anywhere. An address set in a Secret is learned only from the request it’s used for | | ## What it writes | Resource | What | | -------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | Deployments, StatefulSets | Replicas, through the scale subresource. While paused, the `napwake.dev/paused-from` annotation, a copy of what the pause changed, removed on hand-back. No other field, and never a delete | | Its own Deployments, with `doorman.negForwarder.enabled` | A [forwarder](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#gke-container-native-load-balancing-negs), `napwake-forwarder-<workload>`, in the namespace of each paused workload whose Service is behind GKE container-native load balancing. Owned by its ManagedWorkload, deleted a minute after the workload wakes | | KEDA ScaledObjects | `autoscaling.keda.sh/paused-replicas`, while their workload is paused or resuming, then its old value, or none | | EndpointSlices | Its own, routing a paused workload’s Services to the doorman, deleted when it wakes | | ManagedWorkloads | Status, with each workload’s forecast state. The ones it creates for labelled workloads. The `napwake.dev/cleanup` finalizer, so deleting one restores its workload first, removed once it has. `napwake.dev/operator`, its namespace. `napwake.dev/last-activity` on a dependency it wakes | | ManagedWorkloads, by the doorman | `napwake.dev/last-request` and `napwake.dev/last-request-from` (the caller’s IP address), on one a request wakes. `napwake.dev/turned-away`, a count of the requests it [turned away](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#what-it-turns-away) from one that asks, with nothing of the requests themselves | | Its wake key Secret, by the doorman | A random key, when it’s empty. Nothing else | | Events | On ManagedWorkloads and workloads, saying what it did and why | | Leases | Leader election, in its own namespace | | Anything else | **Never.** No other resource or workload field. It deletes only its own ManagedWorkloads, EndpointSlices and forwarder Deployments | ## What it sends | To | What | | ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | The Kubernetes API server | Everything above, always | | Prometheus, at `prometheus.url` | Only if set. The activity queries workloads configure. Once for each new ManagedWorkload, a range query for up to four weeks of its own containers’ CPU (`container_cpu_usage_seconds_total`), an hour a point, to start its forecast from, narrowed by `prometheus.selector` if set | | The woken pod, from the doorman | The requests it held. Their contents are never stored or logged | | The doorman, from a forwarder | Connections, passed on, never stored or logged. A forwarder reads nothing from the API server | | Anywhere else | Nothing. No telemetry, usage reporting or update checks | ## RBAC What the Helm chart grants a release named `napwake`. Resource names start with the release’s full name. Rules marked *namespaced* go in ClusterRoles, or with `watchNamespaces`, only in a Role in each watched namespace. ### Operator ServiceAccount `napwake`, with ClusterRole and ClusterRoleBinding Helm hooks, like their Jobs, existing only while `helm uninstall` runs its pre-delete hook, or `helm upgrade` or `helm rollback` its pre-upgrade or pre-rollback hook. Those refuse to leave ManagedWorkloads without an operator | | People | ClusterRoles `napwake-view` and `napwake-edit` | | Nothing to Napwake itself. Aggregated into the built-in roles, so `view`, `edit` and `admin` gain `get`, `list` and `watch` on `napwake.dev` `managedworkloads`, and `get` on their status. `edit` and `admin` also gain `create`, `update`, `patch` and `delete` | | Admission | ValidatingAdmissionPolicy and binding `napwake-doorman`, cluster-scoped | `doorman.admissionPolicy.enabled` (the default) | Refuses any change the doorman’s ServiceAccount makes to a ManagedWorkload, except to its `napwake.dev/last-request`, `last-request-from` and `turned-away` annotations | | Admission | ValidatingAdmissionPolicy and binding `napwake-request-stamps`, cluster-scoped | `doorman.admissionPolicy.enabled` | Refuses anyone else setting those two annotations. Limited to `watchNamespaces` if set | | Metrics | ClusterRoleBindings `napwake-auth-delegator` and `napwake-doorman-auth-delegator`, to the built-in `system:auth-delegator` ClusterRole | `metrics.enabled` and `metrics.secure` (the defaults) | Lets the operator and the doorman check the tokens of whoever scrapes them | | Metrics | ClusterRole `napwake-metrics-reader`, and a ClusterRoleBinding of that name | `metrics.enabled` and `metrics.secure` | `get` on the `/metrics` URL, bound to `metrics.readerSubjects` | ## The plugin `kubectl napwake` uses your own kubeconfig and permissions. It reads the same kinds of resources and their events, and changes only these. | Command | Changes | | ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `wake` | `napwake.dev/wake-requested` on a ManagedWorkload, with `napwake.dev/active-until` for `--for` | | `pause` | `napwake.dev/pause-requested` on a ManagedWorkload, with `napwake.dev/pause-overrides-forecast` to pause one the forecast expects demand for. With `--clear-hold`, it removes its `napwake.dev/active-until` | | `enable` | `napwake.dev/dry-run`, on a workload or its namespace, or `spec.dryRun` on a ManagedWorkload you wrote | | `scan`, `status --html` | Nothing in the cluster. The report or page goes only to your machine, in a file only you can read. `scan` reads Prometheus through the API server’s service proxy, or at `--prometheus-url` | For a workload Argo CD applies, `scan`, `status`, `pause` and `enable` read Argo CD’s Applications and its `argocd-cm` ConfigMap, to tell whether it leaves the replicas to Napwake.

# Helm values reference

> Every value of the Napwake Helm chart, with its default and what it does.

napwake-values.yaml 
```yaml
timezone: Europe/London
```
 Put the values you set in a file, starting with your users’ [time zone](/next/getting-started/installation/#time-zone). 
```bash
helm install napwake oci://ghcr.io/napwake/charts/napwake --version 0.2.0-rc.9 \
  --namespace napwake-system --create-namespace \
  -f napwake-values.yaml
```
 Install with it, and upgrade with it to [keep your settings](/next/operations/upgrading/#keeping-your-settings). 
```bash
helm show values oci://ghcr.io/napwake/charts/napwake --version 0.2.0-rc.9
```

| | ------------------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | | `nameOverride` | `""` | Replaces the chart name in resource names | | `fullnameOverride` | `""` | Replaces the full name the chart’s resources are named after | The names and labels the chart gives. | | | | -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | Full name | The release name if it contains `napwake`, else `<release>-napwake`, cut to 40 characters so names built from it, the longest being `<fullname>-doorman-auth-delegator`, fit in 63 | | Operator Deployment and ServiceAccount | `napwake`, for a release named `napwake` | | The doorman’s | `napwake-doorman` | | Operator pod labels | `app.kubernetes.io/name: napwake`, `app.kubernetes.io/instance: <release>`, `control-plane: controller-manager` | | Doorman pod labels | The same, with `control-plane: doorman` | ## Image | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | | `image.repository` | `ghcr.io/napwake/napwake` | Container image repository | | `image.tag` | `""`, the chart’s `appVersion`, such as `v0.2.0-rc.9` | Image tag, for the operator and the doorman | | `image.pullPolicy` | `IfNotPresent` | Image pull policy | | `imagePullSecrets` | `[]` | Private registry credentials | ## CRD | Value | Default | Description | | -------------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `crds.install` | `true` | Install the ManagedWorkload CRD and upgrade it with each `helm upgrade`. Set `crds.install: false` only if you manage it, such as from `config/crd` in the repository | ## Defaults version | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------- | ----------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `defaultsVersion` | the chart’s version, such as `0.2.0-rc.9` | Which chart version Helm’s defaults are from. Leave it. See [Keeping your settings](/next/operations/upgrading/#keeping-your-settings) | ## Operator | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `replicaCount` | `1` | Operator replicas. More than one needs `leaderElection.enabled`, or the schema refuses it | | `leaderElection.enabled` | `true` | One replica reconciles, through a Lease. Without it, upgrades stop the old operator before starting the new (`strategy: Recreate`), so two never act at once | | `maxConcurrentReconciles` | `8` | Reconciled at once, so a slow metrics or Prometheus query doesn’t hold up waking another. Sets [`--max-concurrent-reconciles`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-max-concurrent-reconciles) | | `timezone` | `UTC` | IANA zone, such as `Europe/London`, for the [forecast’s](/next/concepts/forecasting/#wall-clock-alignment) hours and weekdays. Use your users’ zone, so forecasts follow daylight saving. Sets [`--timezone`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-timezone) | | `logLevel` | `info` | `debug`, `info`, `error`, `panic`, or a whole number above 0 for more verbose debug levels. Operator and doorman | | `logEncoder` | `json` | `json`, or `console` to read by eye. Operator and doorman | ## Resources | Value | Default | Description | | --------------------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `resources.requests.cpu` | `10m` | CPU request | | `resources.requests.memory` | `128Mi` | Memory request | | `resources.limits.cpu` | `500m` | CPU limit | | `resources.limits.memory` | `512Mi` | Memory limit. Raise it on clusters with many thousands of workloads | ## Namespaces | Value | Default | Description | | --------------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `watchNamespaces` | `[]` | Namespaces Napwake works in, all if empty. Set, it gets a Role in each, not a ClusterRole, so Kubernetes refuses it anything elsewhere ([RBAC](#rbac)). Sets [`--watch-namespaces`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-watch-namespaces), for the doorman too. Each install in a cluster needs its own ([more than one install](/next/getting-started/installation/#more-than-one-install)) | | `protectedNamespaces` | `[]` | Patterns, such as `prod-*`, of namespaces never managed, as if labelled `napwake.dev/protected=true`, unless labelled `napwake.dev/allow-protected=true`. `kube-*` and the operator’s and doorman’s namespace always are, with the same exception ([protected namespaces](/next/guides/opt-in/#protected-namespaces)). Sets [`--protected-namespaces`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-protected-namespaces) | values.yaml 
```yaml
watchNamespaces: [preview-1, preview-2, staging]
protectedNamespaces: ["prod-*", production]
```
 What changes with `watchNamespaces`. * Wake on request still works. The operator always reads the doorman’s EndpointSlices in the release namespace through a `<fullname>-doorman-endpoints` Role. * A `dependsOn` into an unwatched namespace that allows the dependent’s (`napwake.dev/dependents-from`) shows as `DependencyNotFound=True`, reason `DependencyNotVisible`, and never blocks it. * An upgrade or rollback that leaves out a namespace with managed workloads, or sets `watchNamespaces` where it was empty, is refused while they’re there, since they’d stay paused with no operator ([narrowing watchNamespaces](/next/operations/upgrading/#narrowing-watchnamespaces)). ## Opt-in defaults For workloads opted in with the `napwake.dev/managed` label, when neither they nor their namespace set the annotation ([which setting wins](/next/guides/opt-in/#which-setting-wins)). | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------------- | ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `defaults.idleAfter` | `1h` | Time without activity before pausing, a Go duration of at least `1m`, such as `90s` or `1h30m`. Sets [`--default-idle-after`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-default-idle-after) | | `defaults.cpuThreshold` | `10` | CPU % of requests, from 0.1 to 100 to at most two decimal places, such as `10` or `0.5`, above which it’s active. Sets [`--default-cpu-threshold`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-default-cpu-threshold) | | `defaults.dryRun` | `false` | Measure without pausing. `true` rolls Napwake out across a cluster measuring first. Sets [`--default-dry-run`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-default-dry-run) | | `defaults.pauseEarly` | `false` | Let a confident forecast of quiet hours [pause before `idleAfter`](/next/concepts/forecasting/#pausing-early) runs out. Sets [`--default-pause-early`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-default-pause-early) | | `defaults.forecast` | `balanced` | How soon the forecast acts, `eager`, `balanced` or `cautious` ([Forecast setting](/next/concepts/forecasting/#forecast-setting)). Sets [`--default-forecast`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-default-forecast) | | `defaults.wake` | `request` | What wakes a paused workload through the doorman. `request` is any request. `ask` is only a person’s click on **Wake it**, for a [public URL](/next/guides/preview-environments/). Sets [`--default-wake`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-default-wake) | ## Prometheus activity | Value | Default | Description | | --------------------- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `prometheus.url` | `""` | Prometheus API base URL, such as `http://prometheus.monitoring.svc:9090`, for activity queries and to start new workloads’ forecasts from up to four weeks of CPU [history](/next/concepts/forecasting/#learning-from-history). Sets [`--prometheus-url`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-prometheus-url) | | `prometheus.selector` | `""` | Label matchers for the operator’s history query, such as `cluster="staging"`, when Prometheus holds several clusters’ series (Thanos, Mimir, a shared one). Written as inside PromQL’s braces, as `kubectl napwake scan --prometheus-selector` takes them. Activity queries run as written. Needs `prometheus.url`. Sets [`--prometheus-selector`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-prometheus-selector) | ## Metrics | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------------ | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `metrics.enabled` | `true` | Metrics from the operator and the doorman. Also binds both to `system:auth-delegator` (`<fullname>-auth-delegator`, `<fullname>-doorman-auth-delegator`), to check scrapers’ tokens | | `metrics.port` | `8443` | Operator and doorman. Not 8081, the probes’, nor 20000-29999, the doorman’s ports for paused workloads’ traffic | | `metrics.secure` | `true` | HTTPS, only to callers allowed to `get` `/metrics`. `false` serves plain HTTP to anyone who reaches the port | | `metrics.readerSubjects` | `[]` | Subjects, such as your Prometheus’s ServiceAccount, bound to `<fullname>-metrics-reader`, a ClusterRole that may get `/metrics`. kube-prometheus-stack’s is `<its release name>-kube-prometheus-prometheus` | With `metrics.secure`, bind your Prometheus here or with a binding of your own: values.yaml 
```yaml
metrics:
  readerSubjects:
    - kind: ServiceAccount
      name: prometheus-k8s
      namespace: monitoring
```
 ### ServiceMonitor | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------------------------------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `metrics.serviceMonitor.enabled` | `false` | Prometheus Operator ServiceMonitors for the operator and the doorman, with `honorLabels: true` so metrics keep the workload’s `namespace` label ([scraping](/next/operations/monitoring/#scraping)) | | `metrics.serviceMonitor.interval` | `30s` | Scrape interval | | `metrics.serviceMonitor.additionalLabels` | `{}` | Extra labels. Prometheus picks the ServiceMonitors up only if they match its `serviceMonitorSelector`. kube-prometheus-stack needs `release: <its release name>` | ### PrometheusRule | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------------------------------- | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | | `metrics.prometheusRule.enabled` | `false` | A PrometheusRule with the [alerts](/next/operations/monitoring/#alerting-rules). `NapwakeDown` and `NapwakeDoormanDown` match the ServiceMonitors’ scrape jobs, so they’re included only with `metrics.serviceMonitor.enabled` | | `metrics.prometheusRule.additionalLabels` | `{}` | Extra labels. Prometheus loads the rule only if they match its `ruleSelector`. kube-prometheus-stack needs `release: <its release name>` | ## Doorman The doorman holds requests to paused workloads and wakes them ([Wake on request](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/)). It uses the operator’s image, `metrics.*`, `logLevel` and `logEncoder`. | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------------------------------------ | ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `doorman.enabled` | `true` | Deploy the doorman. With `false`, requests to paused workloads fail | | `doorman.replicaCount` | `2` | Replicas, each serving traffic | | `doorman.portsPerWorkload` | `32` | Most ports one workload may hold, one per routed Service port, 1 to 10000, so one tenant can’t take all 10,000 the cluster shares. Over it, it isn’t routed (`WakeOnRequest=False`, reason `TooManyPorts`). Sets [`--doorman-ports-per-workload`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-doorman-ports-per-workload) | | `doorman.portsPerNamespace` | `256` | Most ports one namespace’s workloads may hold together, 1 to 10000. Sets [`--doorman-ports-per-namespace`](/next/reference/configuration/#flag-doorman-ports-per-namespace) | | `doorman.healthCheckUserAgents` | `[]` | Health checkers’ and scrapers’ `User-Agent` prefixes, or names after `(compatible; `in a browser-style one, whose `GET` and `HEAD` don’t wake a workload, such as `["MyCorpMonitor/"]`. The built-in ones are `kube-probe`, Prometheus, load balancers’ checks, `Blackbox Exporter/`, `Datadog/Synthetics`, `Amazon-Route53-Health-Check-Service`, `GoogleStackdriverMonitoring-UptimeChecks`, `UptimeRobot`, `Pingdom.com_bot`, `NewRelicPinger/`, `Uptime-Kuma/`, `Gatus/` and others. See [What wakes a workload](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#what-wakes-a-workload-and-what-doesnt) | | `doorman.admissionPolicy.enabled` | `true` | Two ValidatingAdmissionPolicies. The doorman’s ServiceAccount may change only `napwake.dev/last-request`, `napwake.dev/last-request-from` and `napwake.dev/turned-away` on ManagedWorkloads, though its role may patch them. No one else may set the first two, so a forged `last-request-from` can’t teach the operator a dependency. See [Security](/next/reference/security/#the-doorman-is-a-way-in-to-paused-workloads) | | `doorman.podDisruptionBudget.enabled` | `true` | Keeps one doorman serving through node drains, when `replicaCount` is above 1 | | `doorman.nodeSelector` | `{}` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `doorman.tolerations` | `[]` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `doorman.affinity` | `{}` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `doorman.topologySpreadConstraints` | `[]` | Empty spreads them across nodes and zones where it can | | `doorman.podAnnotations` | `{}` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `doorman.podLabels` | `{}` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `doorman.priorityClassName` | `""` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `doorman.extraEnv` | `[]` | Extra environment variables. The operator’s `extraEnv` doesn’t apply | | `doorman.resources.requests.cpu` | `10m` | CPU request | | `doorman.resources.requests.memory` | `64Mi` | Memory request | | `doorman.resources.limits.cpu` | `500m` | CPU limit | | `doorman.resources.limits.memory` | `256Mi` | Memory limit, enough for some 100,000 pods. Raise it beyond | | `doorman.negForwarder.enabled` | `false` | Optional, for GKE. Routes paused workloads’ Services behind container-native load balancing (NEGs), as a GKE Ingress’s or Gateway’s are, through a forwarder Deployment the operator runs in the workload’s namespace while it’s paused, so requests through the load balancer wake them. Grants the operator `create` and `delete` on Deployments ([RBAC](#rbac)), adds a headless Service `<fullname>-doorman-pods`, and needs `doorman.enabled`. See [GKE NEGs](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#gke-container-native-load-balancing-negs) | | `doorman.negForwarder.replicas` | `2` | Pods per forwarder, spread across nodes so one stays while a node drains | | `doorman.negForwarder.resources.requests.cpu` | `10m` | CPU request, per pod | | `doorman.negForwarder.resources.requests.memory` | `32Mi` | Memory request, per pod | | `doorman.negForwarder.resources.limits.memory` | `64Mi` | Memory limit, per pod | ## Network policy | Value | Default | Description | | --------------------------------------------- | ------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `networkPolicy.enabled` | `false` | NetworkPolicies for the operator and the doorman | | `doorman.networkPolicy.ingressFrom` | `[]` | Peers allowed to reach paused workloads through the doorman, such as your ingress controller’s namespace. Empty leaves the doorman open to every caller. Needs `networkPolicy.enabled` | | `doorman.networkPolicy.egress.enabled` | `false` | An egress policy letting the doorman connect only to `podCIDRs` and the API server. Separate from `networkPolicy.enabled`, and needs a CNI that enforces egress. The doorman needs no DNS, reaching the API server and pods by address | | `doorman.networkPolicy.egress.podCIDRs` | `[]` | Pod CIDRs, in every IP family. Add node CIDRs if a routed workload uses `hostNetwork`. Required when enabled | | `doorman.networkPolicy.egress.apiServer` | `[]` | The API server’s endpoint CIDRs (`kubectl get endpointslices -n default -l kubernetes.io/service-name=kubernetes`), not the `kubernetes` Service’s ClusterIP. Required when enabled | | `doorman.networkPolicy.egress.apiServerPorts` | `[443, 6443]` | Their ports | What the policies admit, with `networkPolicy.enabled`. | Port | Admits | | --------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | The operator’s and the doorman’s metrics port | Only namespaces labelled `metrics: enabled` | | The doorman’s routing ports, TCP 20000-29999 | Every source, since every client of a paused workload’s Services is sent there. With `ingressFrom` and `doorman.negForwarder.enabled`, the forwarders too, from any namespace. To narrow it, see [restricting who can reach the doorman](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#restricting-who-can-reach-the-doorman) | On Cilium An `ipBlock` may not match the API server, which Cilium knows as the `kube-apiserver` entity, not by address. The egress policy can then cut the doorman off, and its pods fail readiness and log API errors. Leave `doorman.networkPolicy.egress.enabled` off, and write a CiliumNetworkPolicy allowing egress `toEntities: [kube-apiserver]` and `toCIDR` your pod CIDRs. ## Service account The operator’s ServiceAccount. The doorman always gets its own, `<fullname>-doorman`. | Value | Default | Description | | ----------------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------- | | `serviceAccount.create` | `true` | Create the operator’s ServiceAccount | | `serviceAccount.name` | `""` | Name override. Defaults to the full name | ## Guards Without the operator, a ManagedWorkload’s finalizer never runs. Its workload stays paused at 0 replicas, its Services routed to the doorman. The guards stop Helm first. | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `guard.uninstall` | `true` | Refuse `helm uninstall` while ManagedWorkloads are left, through a pre-delete hook Job, `<fullname>-uninstall-guard`. Argo CD 3.3 and later runs it as PreDelete, blocking Application deletion too. Earlier versions skip it ([uninstall](/next/getting-started/installation/#uninstall)) | | `guard.upgrade` | `true` | Refuse `helm upgrade` or `helm rollback` to a `watchNamespaces` leaving out namespaces with managed workloads, through a pre-upgrade and pre-rollback hook Job, `<fullname>-upgrade-guard`, only with `watchNamespaces` set. Argo CD runs it as PreSync, before every sync ([narrowing](/next/operations/upgrading/#narrowing-watchnamespaces)) | | `guard.nodeSelector` | `{}` | The hooks’ node selector. Empty uses the operator’s | | `guard.tolerations` | `[]` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `guard.affinity` | `{}` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `guard.priorityClassName` | `""` | Empty uses the operator’s | | `guard.podAnnotations` | `{}` | Over `sidecar.istio.io/inject: "false"` and `linkerd.io/inject: disabled` | | `guard.podLabels` | `{}` | Over `sidecar.istio.io/inject: "false"`. Can’t replace the chart’s labels | | `guard.resources` | 10m/64Mi requests, 100m/128Mi limits | The hooks’ resources | ## Pod scheduling For the operator, and for the doorman unless its own `doorman.*` value is set. | Value | Default | Description | | ------------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `nodeSelector` | `{}` | Node selector | | `tolerations` | `[]` | Tolerations | | `affinity` | `{}` | Affinity and anti-affinity rules | | `podAnnotations` | `{}` | Pod annotations | | `podLabels` | `{}` | Pod labels. Can’t replace the labels the chart selects pods by | | `priorityClassName` | `""` | PriorityClass. Paused workloads’ requests wait on the doorman and operator, so on a cluster that preempts, give them a class above the workloads they wake | | `extraEnv` | `[]` | Extra environment variables, operator only, as container `env` entries, such as `GOMAXPROCS` | ## Security Fixed by the chart, not values. Override them with Helm post-rendering or Kustomize overlays. | Setting | On the | | ------------------------------------- | --------- | | `runAsNonRoot: true` | Pod | | `seccompProfile.type: RuntimeDefault` | Pod | | `readOnlyRootFilesystem: true` | Container | | `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false` | Container | | `capabilities.drop: ["ALL"]` | Container | ## Health probes | Pod | Probe | Path | Port | Initial Delay | Period | | -------- | --------- | ---------- | ---- | ------------- | ------ | | Operator | Liveness | `/healthz` | 8081 | 15s | 20s | | Operator | Readiness | `/readyz` | 8081 | 5s | 10s | | Doorman | Liveness | `/healthz` | 8081 | 15s | 20s | | Doorman | Readiness | `/readyz` | 8081 | 2s | 5s | ## RBAC What the operator’s ClusterRole, `<fullname>-manager`, grants by default. | Resource | Access | For | | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | ManagedWorkloads | Manage | Including creating them for labelled workloads | | Namespaces | Read | The `napwake.dev/managed` label and settings annotations | | Deployments, StatefulSets | Read, scale (scale subresource), patch | Patching sets and removes `napwake.dev/paused-from` while paused. It can’t delete them, and changes nothing else | | PersistentVolumeClaims | Read | Pricing a paused workload’s storage | | HorizontalPodAutoscalers, KEDA ScaledObjects | Read, and annotate ScaledObjects | Pausing a KEDA workload through KEDA | | Pod metrics, from metrics-server | Read | | | Pods | Read, watch | Pricing what pausing frees, sidecars included, and measuring spare node capacity for [saved](/next/concepts/cost-tracking/#how-saved-is-measured). Cached only if bound to a node and not finished, as node, phase and requests | | Nodes | Read | Pricing at instance types’ list prices, measuring spare capacity. Cached without image lists | | Karpenter’s NodePools, Cluster Autoscaler’s `cluster-autoscaler-status` ConfigMap, Deployments in `kube-system` | List NodePools and Deployments, get the ConfigMap | Reporting the node autoscaler (`napwake_node_autoscaler`). Reads NodePools’ names and consolidation settings, only the rest’s metadata | | ConfigMaps | Get, one at a time, uncached | [Learning dependencies](/next/concepts/dependencies/#learned-dependencies) from those a workload’s environment references. Can’t list or change them | | Events | Create | User-facing status updates | | Services | Read | | | EndpointSlices | Manage | Routing paused workloads to the doorman | The other roles. | Role | Kind, and when | Grants | | ------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | | `<fullname>-doorman` | ClusterRole, for the doorman’s own ServiceAccount | Read ManagedWorkloads and EndpointSlices, get the pods a woken workload’s endpoints name (to check each address is its pod’s), patch ManagedWorkloads to wake them, create Events. With `doorman.admissionPolicy.enabled`, patches only set the two request annotations, and the one counting what it [turned away](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#what-it-turns-away) | | `<fullname>-doorman-wake-key` | Role in the release namespace, for the doorman | `get` and `update` on one Secret of the same name, which the chart creates empty. The doorman fills it with the key it signs the [Wake it button](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#the-wake-it-button) with. It can’t read or list any other | | `<fullname>-leader-election` | Role in the release namespace, with leader election | Leases (`get`, `create`, `update`) and Events | | `<fullname>-doorman-endpoints` | Role in the release namespace, with `watchNamespaces` | Read the doorman’s EndpointSlices | | `<fullname>-forwarder` | ClusterRole, or a Role per watched namespace, with `doorman.negForwarder.enabled` | `create` and `delete` Deployments for the [forwarders](/next/concepts/wake-on-request/#gke-container-native-load-balancing-negs). Deletes only its own, owned by a ManagedWorkload. Forwarders run as the namespace’s `default` ServiceAccount, without its token or API access | | `<fullname>-view` | Aggregated into `view`, `edit`, `admin` | Namespace viewers may `get`, `list`, `watch` its ManagedWorkloads, as `kubectl napwake status` and `scan` do. Kustomize’s `managedworkload-viewer-role` is aggregated alike | | `<fullname>-edit` | Aggregated into `edit`, `admin` | Namespace editors may `create`, `update`, `patch`, `delete` its ManagedWorkloads, as `pause`, `wake` and `enable` do. Kustomize’s `managedworkload-editor-role` is aggregated alike | What changes with `watchNamespaces`. * The operator and the doorman get a Role and RoleBinding in each watched namespace instead. * The operator keeps one ClusterRole, to read Namespaces and Nodes and list Karpenter’s NodePools, which only a ClusterRole can grant. * Workloads elsewhere can’t be read, scaled or opted in. Dependencies on them aren’t held or woken. * With `metrics.enabled` and `metrics.secure`, both keep cluster-wide `system:auth-delegator` bindings, good only for checking scrapers’ tokens. Everything Napwake reads, writes and sends is in [Data and access](/next/reference/data-and-access/). ## Example production configuration values-production.yaml 
```yaml
replicaCount: 2


leaderElection:
  enabled: true


timezone: Europe/London


resources:
  requests:
    cpu: 100m
    memory: 256Mi
  limits:
    cpu: "1"
    memory: 1Gi


metrics:
  enabled: true
  secure: true
  readerSubjects:
    - kind: ServiceAccount
      name: prometheus-k8s
      namespace: monitoring
  serviceMonitor:
    enabled: true
    interval: 15s
  prometheusRule:
    enabled: true


protectedNamespaces: ["prod-*"]


affinity:
  podAntiAffinity:
    preferredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:
      - weight: 100
        podAffinityTerm:
          labelSelector:
            matchLabels:
              app.kubernetes.io/name: napwake
              control-plane: controller-manager
          topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname
```
 `affinity` applies to the doorman too, unless `doorman.affinity` is set. Its replicas spread across nodes by default. 
```bash
helm install napwake oci://ghcr.io/napwake/charts/napwake \
  --version 0.2.0-rc.9 \
  -f values-production.yaml \
  -n napwake-system --create-namespace
```

> Every label, annotation and finalizer Napwake reads or writes, with its values, where it goes and what it does.
